It's more that my mind is just too foggy and tense to actually focus on doing them.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing physical symptoms like muscle tension, restlessness, or difficulty falling or staying asleep?\n\nPatient: Yes, I feel a constant physical tension and restlessness, and I'm completely unable to relax even when I know I'm safe. I don't have any specific issues with my sleep, but when I get startled, my heart pounds and I tremble for a really long time before I can finally calm down.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having intrusive, unwanted memories of a past traumatic event, or do you actively avoid places or situations that remind you of something painful?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really experience intrusive memories of past events. I also don't find myself actively avoiding places or things that remind me of any trauma.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you have a hard time controlling your worries about various aspects of your daily life, such as work, health, or finances?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really have uncontrollable worries about general daily things like my finances or work. It's more that I just feel constantly tense, on high alert, and unable to focus my mind.\n\nDoctor: Have you struggled with these difficulties in focusing, feeling restless, or being easily distracted since you were a child, or is this a new experience that only began four weeks ago?\n\nPatient: No, I never struggled with these focus issues when I was growing up. This is a completely new experience for me that only started about four weeks ago. Before this, my head felt clear and I could concentrate without any problems.\n\nDoctor: Since this started four weeks ago, has there been any subtle change in your life, such as a new relationship, a change in your living environment, or increased pressure at work, even if it didn't feel like a major 'event' at the time?"
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes, these symptoms definitely started right around four weeks ago. However, I really can't think of any subtle changes like a new relationship, moving, or extra pressure at work that could have triggered this. It feels like this fog and tension just came on without any clear cause.

    "role": "system",
    "content": "/no_think\nYou are an assistant analyst connecting the [Symptom Profile] with the doctor's question.\nDetermine which part of the profile (chief complaint, associated symptoms, social history, etc.)\nthe doctor's **last question** is related to.\n\n[Symptom Profile]\n▸ acute_stress_symptoms\n  - Reduced Concentration: Unable to pay attention or maintain focus on specific activities like conversation, reading, or watching TV.\n  - Indecisiveness: Extreme difficulty making even minor daily choices (e.g., menu, clothes) and hesitating to make decisions.\n  - Subjective Memory Complaints: Complaining of memory decline, such as forgetting where things were placed, due to reduced focus.\n  - Clouded Thinking: Thinking feels foggy, dazed, or not clear.\n  - Scanning for Danger: Always scanning the surroundings and being alert to find potential threats or risks.\n  - Safety Checking: Repetitively confirming safety, such as checking exit locations or observing people around.\n  - Sustained Alertness: Unable to relax tension even in safe environments and maintaining a constant state of alertness.\n  - Those physical symptoms like my heart pounding and trembling only really happen after I've been startled, and it just takes a very long time for me to recover and calm down.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you are generally hyper-vigilant or 'on guard' throughout the day, constantly scanning your environment as if you are waiting for something bad to happen?\n\nPatient: Yes, I am constantly on guard and find it impossible to relax, even when I know I'm in a completely safe place. I always find myself scanning my surroundings and looking out for any potential dangers or threats throughout the day."
  }

Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others. Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).  Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.
